\displaystyle\left\{({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{3}})^{2}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{4}})^{2}+\frac{({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{3}})^{2}}{n}+\frac{({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{4}})^{2}}{n}\right\}^{1/2}+O(1/n)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{3}}\sum_{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}}\left\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{1}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{2}}+\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{1}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{2}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}\left\{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{3}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{4}}+\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{3}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p_{4}}}{\sqrt{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle+O(1/n)

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{n^{3}}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{4}+4\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+4\frac{m^{2}}{n}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}_{j,j}\right)+O(1/n),

where we used the inequality
\sqrt{a+b+c}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}+\sqrt{c}
for non-negative numbers
a,b,c
.

Consider now
{\rm E}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{m}W_{j,p}\right)^{4}
. We have,

{\rm E}(W_{j,p_{1}}W_{j,p_{2}}W_{j,p_{3}}W_{j,p_{4}})=\frac{1}{n^{8}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}{\rm E}\Big[\left\{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{1},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{1},p_{1}}^{2}-E\left({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{1},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{1},p_{1}}^{2}\right)\right\}\\
\left\{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{2},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{2},p_{2}}^{2}-{\rm E}\left({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{2},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{2},p_{2}}^{2}\right)\right\}\left\{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{3},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{3},p_{3}}^{2}-{\rm E}\left({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{3},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{3},p_{3}}^{2}\right)\right\}\\
\left\{{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{4},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{4},p_{4}}^{2}-{\rm E}\left({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{4},j}^{2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{4},p_{4}}^{2}\right)\right\}\Big]=O(1/n^{6}),

where the last equality is true because the expectation is different from zero when
i_{1}=i_{2}
,
i_{3}=i_{4}
(or symmetric cases), and there are order of
n^{2}
such cases. Therefore,

{\rm E}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{m}W_{j,p}\right)^{4}=\sum_{p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4}}{\rm E}(W_{j,p_{1}}W_{j,p_{2}}W_{j,p_{3}}W_{j,p_{4}})=O(1/n^{2}).

To sum up,

{\rm E}\left(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{4}\right)\leq{C}{\rm E}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{m}V_{j,p}\right)^{4}+{C}{\rm E}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{m}W_{j,p}\right)^{4}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3}}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{4}+4\frac{m}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+4\frac{m^{2}}{n}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2}_{j,j}\right)+O(1/n);

notice that the constant
C
is not the same in the first and second inequalities.
This completes the proof of the Lemma. ∎

C.8
Proof of Theorem
6

Proof of Part (i):

Recall that
\mathaccentV{bar}016{D}_{\rm GWASH}:=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}
. Since
\ell_{j}\geq 1
,

(\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}-\ell_{j})^{2}\leq(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\ell_{j})^{2}
almost surely. It follows that

{\rm E}\left(\mathaccentV{bar}016{D}_{\rm GWASH}-\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}\right)^{2}\leq{\rm E}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}\right)^{2}.

Because
n/m\to 1/\lambda
and
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}\to\mu_{2}
, to prove (i), it is enough to show that
{\rm E}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mu_{2}/\lambda\right)^{2}
converges to zero.

By (
5
),
{\rm E}(\hat{\ell}_{j,R})=\ell_{j}(1+1/n)
. It follows that

{\rm E}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\right)=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\ell_{j}(1+1/n)\to\frac{\mu_{2}}{\lambda},

where in the latter limit we used the assumptions
m/n\to\lambda
and
\frac{1}{r(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\to\mu_{2}
(recalling that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}={\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
).
In order to show convergence in
{\cal L}_{2}
, we need to consider the variance of
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}
. By Lemma
3
,

{\rm Var}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\leq\frac{C}{m[r(m)]^{2}}\left({\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})+\frac{1}{m}\left[{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right]^{2}+{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})\right).

(52)

We next show that this bound converges to zero. Let
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\cdots\geq\lambda_{n}
denote the eigenvalues of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
. We have that

\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}}{r(m)}\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{2}}{r(m)}\leq C.

Therefore,
\lambda_{1}^{2}\leq Cr(m)
. It follows that

\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{4}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{2}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\leq C\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{2}}{r(m)}\to C\mu_{2}

and therefore
\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}
is bounded, which implies that the term
\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{m[r(m)]^{2}}
in (
52
) converges to zero. Also,

\frac{1}{m^{2}[r(m)]^{2}}\left[{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right]^{2}=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\left[{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})/r(m)\right]^{2}\to 0,

because
{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})/r(m)\to\mu_{2}
. Finally,

\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})}{m[r(m)]^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{3}}{m[r(m)]^{2}}\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}^{2}}{m[r(m)]^{2}}=\frac{\lambda_{1}}{mr(m)}\cdot\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)};

now
\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)}
is bounded and
\frac{\lambda_{1}}{mr(m)}
converges to zero because
\lambda_{1}\leq m
. It follows that the bound in (
52
) converges to zero.

Proof of Part (ii):
By (
6
),
E(u_{j}^{2}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\frac{m}{n}\right)+d_{j}^{2}
. Therefore,

{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\frac{m}{n}\right)+\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(d_{j}^{2}-1\right).

(53)

Now, we have that
{\rm E}(d_{j}^{2})=1
and
E\left(\hat{\ell}_{j}-\frac{m}{n}\right)=\ell_{j}(1+1/n)
. Hence,

{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH})={\rm E}[{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})]=h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}(1+1/n)\to h^{2}\mu_{2}/\lambda.

Proof of Part (iii):

We have the conditional variance decomposition

{\rm Var}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH})={\rm Var}[{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})]+{\rm E}[{\rm Var}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})].

(54)

From (
53
),
{\rm Var}[{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})]
is the variance of the sum of two terms:

{\rm Var}[{\rm E}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})]={\rm Var}\left[h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\frac{m}{n}\right)+\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(d_{j}^{2}-1\right)\right].

This variance goes to zero because

{\rm Var}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}^{2}\right)=\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}{\rm Cov}(d_{j}^{2},d_{j^{\prime}}^{2})=\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}n}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{ij}^{2},{\boldsymbol{X}}_{ij^{\prime}}^{2})\\
=\frac{2}{[r(m)]^{2}n}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{2}=\frac{2{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{[r(m)]^{2}n}\to 0,

and we already showed in (
52
) that
{\rm Var}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\to 0
.

Consider now the term
{\rm E}[{\rm Var}(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}})]
in (
54
). By (
12
), appropriately replacing
m
by
r(m)
as a dividing factor,

{\rm E}[{\rm Var}\left(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)]=\frac{1}{r(m)}\left[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3\right]\frac{h^{4}n^{2}}{m^{2}}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}E(\hat{\ell}_{j}^{2})\\
+\left[{\rm Kurt}(\varepsilon_{i})-3\right]\frac{(1-h^{2})^{2}}{n}\frac{m^{2}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\rm E}\left[\left(\frac{\|\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{i}\|^{2}}{m}\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}\left[{\rm E}\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\boldsymbol{S}^{2}+(1-h^{2})\boldsymbol{S}\right)^{2}\right].

(55)

Consider the first term in (
55
). We have

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm E}(\hat{\ell}_{j}^{2})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\{[{\rm E}(\hat{\ell}_{j})]^{2}+{\rm Var}(\hat{\ell}_{j})\right\}.

By (
5
),
{\rm E}(\hat{\ell}_{j})=\ell_{j}(1+1/n)+\frac{m}{n}
. Therefore,

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}[{\rm E}(\hat{\ell}_{j})]^{2}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}^{2}+\frac{O(m)}{r(m)}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}^{2}+O(1).

Also, by (
32
),

{\rm Var}(\hat{\ell}_{j})\leq\frac{4}{n}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+\ell_{j}^{2})+\frac{2}{n^{2}}({\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})+\ell_{j}^{2})+\frac{8}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+m\ell_{j})+O(1/n).

Therefore,

\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm Var}(\hat{\ell}_{j})\leq\frac{C}{m}\left[{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})+\sum_{j}\ell_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{m}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right].

Notice that
{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})\leq\lambda_{1}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\leq m{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
and
\sum_{j}\ell_{j}^{2}\leq m\sum_{j}\ell_{j}
because
\ell_{j}\leq m
. Hence,
\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm Var}(\hat{\ell}_{j})=O(m)
. It follows that
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}E(\hat{\ell}_{j}^{2})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}^{2}+O(1)
.

The second term in (
55
)
goes to 0 because the factor

\left[{\rm Kurt}(\varepsilon_{i})-3\right]\frac{m^{2}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\rm E}\left[\left(\frac{\|\vec{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{i}\|^{2}}{m}\right)^{2}\right]

multiplying
(1-h^{2})^{2}/n
is bounded.

Consider now the last term in (
55
). By by the equations given on page 99 of
Gupta and Nagar (2018)
we have that this term,

\frac{2}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}\left[{\rm E}\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\boldsymbol{S}^{2}+(1-h^{2})\boldsymbol{S}\right)^{2}\right],

is of the form

\frac{a}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})+\frac{b}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})+\frac{c}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})

for some constants
a,b,c
. We have that
\frac{a}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
converges to zero because we assumed that
\frac{1}{r(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\to\mu_{2}
. Also,

\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})\leq\frac{\lambda_{1}}{r(m)}\cdot\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)};

\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)}
is bounded and
\frac{\lambda_{1}}{r(m)}\to 0
because
\lambda_{1}\leq C\sqrt{r(m)}
(see the argument after (
52
)). It follows that
\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})\to 0
. Therefore,

\frac{2}{[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}\left[{\rm E}\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\boldsymbol{S}^{2}+(1-h^{2})\boldsymbol{S}\right)^{2}\right]=O(1)\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}.

We conclude that

{\rm E}[{\rm Var}\left(\mathaccentV{bar}016{N}_{\rm GWASH}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)]=\frac{1}{r(m)}\left[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3\right]\left[\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\ell_{j}^{2}+O(1)\right]+O(1)\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}.

∎

C.9
Proof of Theorem
7

From the definition (
23
) of
E_{j}
, we can write

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(E_{j}-1)}{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left[\left(\frac{{\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}-1\right)-\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{2}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}+\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right){\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{3}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}\right]}{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}}

(56)

\displaystyle=\frac{({\rm I})-({\rm II})+({\rm III})}{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}}.

We work on the three terms one at a time.

Consider term (I). By Lemma
7
below,

\frac{{\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}-1=\frac{h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)+d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)}-1.

Now,
{\rm E}\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)=1
because
{\rm E}\left({\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})\right)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm E}(d_{j}^{2})=m
. Also,

{\rm Var}\left({\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j,k}{\rm Cov}(d_{j}^{2},d_{k}^{2})\leq\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j,k}\left\{{\rm Var}(d_{j}^{2}){\rm Var}(d_{k}^{2})\right\}^{1/2}\leq C/n,

where the last inequality is true because
{\rm Var}(d_{j}^{2})={\rm Var}(X_{ij}^{2})/n\leq C/n
(assuming boundedness of the forth moment). It follows that as
n,m\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{\left[1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right]^{2}}=1+o_{p}(1).

Hence,

({\rm I})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{{\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}-1\right)=\frac{h^{2}[1+o_{p}(1)]}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}-1\right).

Now, the expression inside the parentheses can be written as

\frac{\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}+\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}\left(\frac{d_{j}^{2}-1}{d_{j}^{2}}\right).

(57)

Continuing with the latter term, we have

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}\left(\frac{d_{j}^{2}-1}{d_{j}^{2}}\right)\leq C\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}\left(d_{j}^{2}-1\right).

By Lemma
8
below, the latter term converges in probability to zero because Lemma
9
(below) implies that
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges in probability to a finite limit, and by (
66
) below,

P\left(\{|d_{1}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cup\{|d_{2}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cdots\{|d_{m}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\right)\leq C\varepsilon^{4}/m.

Hence,
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}\left|d_{j}^{2}-1\right|
converges in probability to zero.
We conclude that

({\rm I})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{{\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}-1\right)=\frac{h^{2}[1+o_{p}(1)]}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-1\right).

(58)

Going back to (
56
), consider now the term (II). We have by Lemma
7
below,

\displaystyle({\rm II})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{2}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\bigg\{\frac{n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}+\frac{2n{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}h^{4}/m^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\frac{1}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{1}}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}+\frac{\frac{2}{n^{2}}d_{j}^{2}(1-h^{2})^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}+\frac{4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)^{2}}\bigg\{({\rm IIa})+({\rm IIb})+({\rm IIc})+({\rm IId})+({\rm IIe})\bigg\}.

(59)

Begin with

({\rm IIa})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}=n[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}.

Continuing with the sum, since
\hat{\ell}_{j}=\sum_{p=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,p}^{2}
,

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,p}\frac{{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,p}^{2}d_{p}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,p}\frac{{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,p}^{2}(d_{j}^{2}+d_{p}^{2}-d_{j}^{2})}{d_{j}^{2}}\\
=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}+\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,p}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,p}^{2}\frac{d_{p}^{2}-d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}.

By similar arguments as before using Lemma
8
we have that
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,p}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,p}^{2}\frac{d_{p}^{2}-d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}
converges in probability to zero. Therefore,

({\rm IIa})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[{\rm E}(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}=n[{\rm E}(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}+o_{p}(1)\right).

Notice that Lemma
9
implies that

\frac{1}{r(m)}\frac{n}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\frac{n}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j}-{\frac{m}{n}}\right)+{\frac{m}{r(m)}}=\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+{\frac{m}{r(m)}}+o_{p}(1).

(60)

Hence,

({\rm IIa})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[{\rm E}(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}=m[{\rm E}(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]\left(\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+{\frac{m}{r(m)}}+o_{p}(1)\right)\\
=\frac{{h^{4}}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]\left(\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+{\frac{m}{r(m)}}+o_{p}(1)\right).

(61)

Consider now

({\rm IIb})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2n{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}h^{4}/m^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}.

Notice that
{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}={\boldsymbol{e}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{S}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{e}}_{j}=\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}
.
Thus,

({\rm IIb})=\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}}+\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}}\frac{1-d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}.

By Lemma
8
the last term converges in probability to zero if
\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}}
can be bounded with high probability. Let
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\geq\hat{\lambda}_{2}\geq\cdots\geq\hat{\lambda}_{m}
be the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{S}
. We have that

\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2}}{r(m)}\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}{r(m)}=\frac{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})}{r(m)}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j},

and the latter term converges in probability. It follows that there exists
C
such that
P\left(\hat{\lambda}_{1}\leq C\sqrt{r(m)}\right)\to 1
. Now,

\frac{1}{mr(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}}=\frac{1}{mr(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{3}}\leq\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}}{m}\cdot\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}.

The term
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}
converges in probability and we now argue that
\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}}{m}
is bounded in probability. Indeed, with probability converging to one,
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\leq C\sqrt{r(m)}
and hence, with high probability
\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}}{m}
is bounded as
r(m)\leq m^{2}
. We conclude that

\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}}\frac{1-d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

and hence,

({\rm IIb})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2n{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}h^{4}/m^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})+o_{p}(1).

(62)

The next term to consider is

({\rm IIc})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\frac{1}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{1}}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}=[{\rm E}(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]\frac{1}{r(m)n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\|{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\|^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}.

We have that
\frac{1}{d_{j}^{2}}\leq C
and
{\rm E}\left(\|{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}\|^{2}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}^{2})\leq Cn
.
It follows that

{\rm E}\left|({\rm IIc})\right|={\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\frac{1}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{1}}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]}{d_{j}^{2}}\right|\leq C\frac{m}{nr(m)}\to 0,

and therefore (IIc) converges in probability to zero.

The next term is

({\rm IId})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{2}{n^{2}}(1-h^{2})^{2}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\frac{2m}{n^{2}}(1-h^{2})^{2}\to 0.

Finally, the last term is

({\rm IIe})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{4(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{mr(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{mr(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\hat{\ell}_{j}},

which converges in probability to zero because
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\hat{\ell}_{j}}
converges in probability to a finite limit.

Putting together the five terms in (
59
), we conclude that

\displaystyle({\rm II})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{2}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}

(63)

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{h^{4}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]\left(\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+{\frac{m}{r(m)}}\right)+\frac{2nh^{4}}{m^{2}r(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})\right\}\{1+o_{p}(1)\}.

Going back to (
56
), consider now
({\rm III})
. We have by Lemma
7
that

({\rm III})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right){\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{3}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}\\
=\frac{1}{\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)^{3}}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)+d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}\times\\
\left\{({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})h^{4}/m^{2}+\frac{1}{n}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]\right.\\
\left.+\frac{2}{n}(1-h^{2})^{2}+4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{nm}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})\right\}

By similar arguments as before we have that

\frac{1}{\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right)^{3}}=1+o_{p}(1)

and the term

\frac{1}{n}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]+\frac{2}{n}(1-h^{2})^{2}+4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{nm}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})

is negligible. It follows that

({\rm III})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right){\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{3}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}=\{1+o_{p}(1)\}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)+d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}\times\\
\left\{({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})h^{4}/m^{2}+o_{p}(1).\right\}

Notice that

({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}^{2}=m(1+o_{p})
, and therefore
({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]
in the last term is equal to

({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]=m[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+o_{p}(1)={\frac{h^{4}}{m}}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+o_{p}(1).

Since by (
57
),

\frac{\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}+o_{p}(1),

it follows that

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)+d_{j}^{2}}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-1\right)+\frac{m}{r(m)}+o_{p}(1)

which, by (
60
) equals to
{h^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}+o_{p}(1)}
. We conclude that

({\rm III})=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right){\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{3}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}=\left[{h^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}}+o_{p}(1)\right]\times\\
\left\{{\frac{h^{4}}{m}}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+2\frac{h^{4}}{m^{2}}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})+o_{p}(1)\right\}

Putting all the terms in (
56
) one obtains (using Lemmas
9
and
10
for the denominator) that

\frac{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(E_{j}-1)}{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}}=h^{2}+\frac{1}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]{h^{4}}(h^{2}-1)\\
+\frac{2h^{4}}{m^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda}\left[\left({h^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}}\right){\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})-\frac{n}{r(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})\right]+o_{p}(1).

∎

Lemma 7
.

Under Model (
1
) we have that

\displaystyle E(N_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})

\displaystyle=h^{2}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)+d_{j}^{2},

\displaystyle{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)

\displaystyle=d_{j}^{2}\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right),

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{d_{j}^{2}}

\displaystyle=n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2n{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}h^{4}/m^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{1}}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]+\frac{2}{n^{2}}d_{j}^{2}(1-h^{2})^{2}+4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{m},

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{d_{j}^{4}}

\displaystyle=({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})h^{4}/m^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]+\frac{2}{n}(1-h^{2})^{2}+4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{nm}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}).

Proof of Lemma
7
:

Recall that
N_{u_{j}}=\frac{1}{n}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}})^{2}
, and that
D_{u_{j}}=d_{j}^{2}\frac{1}{n}{\boldsymbol{y}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}}
.
E(N_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})
is given in (
6
). To evaluate
{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)
, we have that

\displaystyle{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})

\displaystyle={\rm E}[{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]={\rm E}[{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}]={\boldsymbol{X}}E[{\boldsymbol{\beta}}]={\bf 0},

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})

\displaystyle={\rm E}[{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]+{\rm Var}[{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}})|{\boldsymbol{X}}]=(1-h^{2})\boldsymbol{I}+\frac{h^{2}}{m}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}=:{\boldsymbol{C}}.

It follows that

{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)=d_{j}^{2}\frac{1}{n}E({\boldsymbol{y}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=d_{j}^{2}\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{C}})=d_{j}^{2}\left(1-h^{2}+h^{2}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S})/m\right).

We next compute
{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)
.
We have

\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{d_{j}^{2}}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}\left(({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}})^{2},{\boldsymbol{y}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)\\
=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+2{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}+{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+2{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}+{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})\\
=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})\\
+\frac{4}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}).

By Lemma
1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=n({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2n{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}\boldsymbol{S}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}h^{4}/m^{2}.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=\frac{1}{n^{2}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{1}}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]+\frac{2}{n^{2}}d_{j}^{2}(1-h^{2})^{2},

where
{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}:=(d_{1}^{2},\ldots,d_{m}^{2})^{T}={\rm Diag}(\boldsymbol{S})
.
The last covariance to compute is

\frac{4}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=\frac{4}{n}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=4(1-h^{2}){\rm E}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{k}{\boldsymbol{r}}_{k}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})\\
=4(1-h^{2})\frac{h^{2}}{m}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T})=4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j}=4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}.

We next compute
{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)
:

\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{d_{j}^{4}}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}\left({\boldsymbol{y}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{y}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)\\
=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Cov}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+2{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}+{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}+2{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}+{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})\\
=\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})+\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})+\frac{4}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}).

By Lemma
1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=({\boldsymbol{d}}^{2})^{T}{\boldsymbol{d}}^{2}[E(\beta_{i}^{4})-3h^{4}/m^{2}]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})h^{4}/m^{2}.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=\frac{1}{n}[E(\varepsilon_{i}^{4})-3(1-h^{2})^{2}]+\frac{2}{n}(1-h^{2})^{2}.

And,

\frac{4}{n^{2}}{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=\frac{4}{n^{2}}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=\frac{4(1-h^{2})}{n^{2}}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}|{\boldsymbol{X}})=4\frac{(1-h^{2})h^{2}}{nm}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}).

∎

Lemma 8
.

Consider two sequences (triangular array) of non-negative random variables
V_{1},\ldots,V_{m},\ldots
,
W_{1},\ldots,W_{m},\ldots
, where
V_{1},\ldots,V_{m}
and
W_{1},\ldots,W_{m}
may depend on
m
. Suppose that there exists a constant
C
such that
P\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}>C\right)\to 0
as
m\to\infty
and that for every
\varepsilon>0

P\left(\{W_{1}>\varepsilon\}\cup\{W_{2}>\varepsilon\}\cdots\cup\{W_{m}>\varepsilon\}\right)\to 0\text{ as }m\to\infty.

Then,
\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}
converges in probability to zero as
m\to\infty
.

Proof of Lemma
8
:
Define an event

E:=\Big\{\{W_{1}\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\{W_{2}\leq\varepsilon\}\cdots\cap\{W_{m}\leq\varepsilon\}\cap\{\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}\leq C\}\Big\};

by the assumptions,
P(E)\to 1
. Write

\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}I(E)+\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}I(E^{c}),

where
I(\cdot)
is the indicator function. When
E
occurs
\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}\leq C\varepsilon
, which is arbitrarily close to zero.
On the other hand,
\sum_{j=1}^{m}V_{j}W_{j}I(E^{c})
converges in probability to zero because
P[I(E^{c})=0]\to 1
. This completes the proof of the lemma. ∎

Lemma 9
.

Suppose that
r(m)
satisfies (
17
), that
M
1
holds, that
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\to\underline{\mu}_{2}
for a constant
\underline{\mu}_{2}
and
\frac{m}{n}\to\lambda>0
, then

E\left[\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda\right]^{2}\to 0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

Proof of Lemma
9
:
Recall that
\underline{\ell}_{j}=E(\hat{\ell}_{j,R})
. Then,

E\left[\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\right]=\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\to\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda.

Furthermore, by Lemma
4
,

{\rm Var}\left(\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\leq{C}\left(\frac{2}{n[r(m)]^{2}}{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})+\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}}\sum_{j,k}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,k}+\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}n^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right).

(64)

To prove the lemma we need to show that this bound goes to zero. Let
\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{1},\ldots,\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{m}
denote the eigenvalues of
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
. We have that

\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{1}^{2}}{r(m)}\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}{r(m)}=\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)}=\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)}\to\mu_{2}.

Therefore,
\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{1}^{2}\leq Cr(m)
. It follows that

\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{j}^{4}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}{[r(m)]^{2}}\leq C\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\lambda_{j}}^{2}}{r(m)}=C\frac{{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}{r(m)}\to C\mu_{2}

and therefore
\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}
is bounded.

The bound in (
64
) goes to zero because
\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{4})}{[r(m)]^{2}}
is bounded and
\frac{1}{r(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\to\mu_{2}
. Notice also that

\frac{1}{[r(m)]^{2}}\sum_{j,k}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,k}\leq\frac{m}{[r(m)]^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{j,k}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,k}^{2}}=\frac{m}{[r(m)]^{3/2}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{r(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})},

and the latter term goes to zero because
m\leq r(m)
and
\frac{1}{r(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\to\mu_{2}
. This completes the proof of the lemma. ∎

Lemma 10
.

Assume that the conditions of Lemma
9
are met and also that
\frac{1}{d_{j}^{2}}\leq C
for all
j
, then

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}-\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

Proof of Lemma
10
:
Recall that

\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}:=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{j,R}^{T}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R}^{T}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{R}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}=\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}.

and that
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}={\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}
.
It follows that

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}-\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}-1\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}+\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}.

(65)

Starting with the second term in the right-hand side of (
65
), we have

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,k}r_{j,k,R}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{d_{k,R}^{2}}-1\right)\\
=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j,k}r_{j,k,R}^{2}\frac{d_{k,R}^{2}-1}{d_{k,R}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{r(m)}\sum_{j,k}r_{j,k,R}^{2}(d_{k,R}^{2}-1)=\frac{C}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}(d_{j,R}^{2}-1).

Consider the bound

{\rm E}\left(d_{j}^{2}-1\right)^{4}=\frac{1}{n^{4}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}E({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{1},j}^{2}-1)({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{2},j}^{2}-1)({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{3},j}^{2}-1)({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i_{4},j}^{2}-1)\leq C/m^{2},

which is true because the expectation is different from zero only when
i_{1}=i_{2}
,
i_{3}=i_{4}
, or symmetric cases. Hence, by Markov’s inequality,

P(|d_{j}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon)=P((d_{j}^{2}-1)^{4}>\varepsilon^{4})\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{4}m^{2}};

therefore, by the union bound,

P\left(\{|d_{1}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cup\{|d_{2}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cup\cdots\cup\{|d_{m}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\right)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}P(|d_{j}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{4}m}.

(66)

Also, by Lemma
9
,
\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges in probability to a finite limit. Therefore, Lemma
8
implies that

\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

Consider now the first term in the right-hand side of (
65
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}-1\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{1-d_{j,R}^{2}}{d_{j,R}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left({1-d_{j,R}^{2}}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R},

by the same arguments as before, the latter term converges in probability to zero. This concludes the proof of the lemma. ∎

C.10
Proof of Corollary
2

The bias term is asymptotically equal to

\frac{1}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]h^{4}(h^{2}-1)+\frac{2h^{4}}{m^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda}\left[\left({h^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}}\right){\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})-\frac{n}{r(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})\right].

The first term is negative when
h^{2}<1
and it converges to zero iff the BKE condition holds.

For the second term, notice that since
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}={\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})
, (
60
) implies that

\frac{n}{mr(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})=\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}+o_{p}(1).

Because
h^{2}\leq 1
we have that

\left({h^{2}\underline{\mu}_{2}/\lambda+\frac{m}{r(m)}}\right){\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})-\frac{n}{r(m)}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})\leq\frac{n}{mr(m)}\left\{\left[{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\right]^{2}-m{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})\right\}+o_{p}(1).

(67)

Let
\hat{\lambda}_{1},\cdots,\hat{\lambda}_{m}
be the eigenvalues of
\boldsymbol{S}
. The term in the curly brackets in (
67
) is (asymptotically) negative because

\left\{\frac{1}{m}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\right\}^{2}=\left\{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}\right\}^{2}=\left\{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{1/2}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{3/2}\right\}^{2}\leq\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}}_{1+o_{p}(1)}\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{3}}_{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})/m},

where the inequality is due to Cauchy-Schwarz. This shows that the second term of the bias is asymptotically non-positive.

Under
WD
1
,
\frac{1}{m^{2}}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges to zero because
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges to a finite limit (see (
60
) and notice that under
WD
1
,
r(m)=m
). Furthermore,

\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2}}{m}\leq\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}{m}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}^{2}}{m},

and the latter term converges in probability to a finite limit. It follows that with probability converging to one,
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\leq C\sqrt{m}
for a constant
C
. Hence,

\frac{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{3})}{m}=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{3}}{m}\leq\frac{\hat{\lambda}_{1}}{m}\cdot\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\lambda}_{j}^{2}}{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Therefore, the second term of the bias converges to zero under
WD
1
. ∎

C.11
Proof of Theorem
8

By Lemmas
11
and
12
below,

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}/\lambda.

The rest of the computations are similar to those in Theorem
7
and only a sketch of the proof is given below.
We have that

\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}(E_{j}-1)}{\frac{1}{r(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}\\
=\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}\left(\frac{{\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}-1-\frac{{\rm Cov}\left({N_{u_{j}}},D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{2}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}+\frac{{\rm Var}\left(D_{u_{j}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right){\rm E}\left({N_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}{{\rm E}^{3}\left({D_{u_{j}}}|{\boldsymbol{X}}\right)}\right)}{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}\\
=h^{2}-\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}\frac{h^{4}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+2n\boldsymbol{S}_{j,j}^{3}\frac{h^{4}}{m^{2}}\right)}{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}\\
+\left(\frac{h^{4}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\frac{h^{4}}{m^{2}}\right)\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}\left[h^{2}(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-1)+1\right]}{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}+o_{p}(1)\\
=h^{2}-\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left(\left[\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right]\frac{h^{4}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+2n\boldsymbol{S}_{j,j}^{3}\frac{h^{4}}{m^{2}}\right)}{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}}\\
+\left(\frac{h^{4}}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]+2{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\frac{h^{4}}{m^{2}}\right)\frac{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left[h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right]}{\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}}+o_{p}(1)\\
=h^{2}+\frac{1}{m}[{\rm Kurt}(\beta_{i})-3]h^{4}(h^{2}-1)+\frac{2h^{4}/m^{2}}{\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}/\lambda}\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left[{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right)-n\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}\right]+o_{p}(1).

∎

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
\tilde{r}(m)
is such that
\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}\to\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}
, that
\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})}{\tilde{r}(m)}
is bounded, that M
2
holds, and that
m/n\to\lambda>0
, then

E\left|\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}-\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}/\lambda\right|\to 0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

Proof of Lemma
11
:

Recall the definition of
\hat{Z}_{j,R}
in (
48
). We have that
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}=\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}
.
Therefore,

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}\right)^{2}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{2}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\hat{Z}_{j,R}+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{2}.

(68)

The first summand in the right-hand side of (
68
) converges to
\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}
, by definition. We next argue that the second and third term converges to zero in
{\cal L}_{1}
, which completes the proof of the lemma since
\frac{n}{m}\to 1/\lambda
.

Consider now the second term. By Lemma
6
(i),

{\rm E}\left(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{2}\right)\leq\frac{C}{n}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+2{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right).

Therefore,

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}E|\hat{Z}_{j,R}|\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\sqrt{E(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{2})}\leq\frac{C}{\tilde{r}(m)\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\sqrt{\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})}\\
\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})]\right)^{1/2},

(69)

where the last inequality is due to Cauchy-Schwarz. We assumed that
\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}=\frac{{\rm tr}(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{3})}{\tilde{r}(m)}
is bounded.
The assumption that
\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}\to\underline{\mu}_{2}^{*}
implies that
\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\ell}_{j}^{2}
is bounded because the difference between
\underline{\ell}_{j}
and
\ell_{j}
is
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\left[{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,p})-1\right]
(see (
14
)), which is bounded.
Also,
\frac{m}{n\tilde{r}(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\leq\frac{C}{\tilde{r}(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})
is bounded because
\tilde{r}(m)\geq r(m)
and
\frac{1}{{r}(m)}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\to\mu_{2}
. It follows that the bound in (
69
) converges to zero.

Consider now the third term in (
69
). Again, by Lemma
6
(i),

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}E(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{2})\leq\frac{C}{n\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}[\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})],

which converges to zero as above. ∎

Lemma 12
.

Assume that the conditions of Lemma
11
are met and also that
\frac{1}{d_{j}^{2}}\leq C
for all
j
, then

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}^{2}_{j,R}-\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}^{2}_{j,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

Proof of Lemma
12
:

Recall that

\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}:=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{j,R}^{T}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R}^{T}\tilde{{\boldsymbol{x}}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{R}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}=\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}.

and that
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}={\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}-\frac{m}{n}
.
It follows that

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}-\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{1}{d_{j,R}^{2}}-1\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\\
+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}.

(70)

Consider the last term

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,k,R}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{d_{k,R}^{2}}-1\right)\\
\leq\frac{C}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,k,R}^{2}\left({d_{k,R}^{2}}-1\right)^{2}.

As in (
66
) we have that

P\left(\{|d_{1}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cup\{|d_{2}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\cup\cdots\cup\{|d_{m}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon\}\right)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}P(|d_{j}^{2}-1|>\varepsilon)\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{4}m}.

Also, by Lemma
11
,

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\sum_{k=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,k,R}^{2}=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}

converges in probability. Hence, by Lemma
8
,

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}{\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}^{T}\left({\boldsymbol{D}}_{R}^{-2}-\boldsymbol{I}\right){\boldsymbol{s}}_{j,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\text{ as }m,n\to\infty.

By similar arguments, the rest of the terms in (
70
) converges to zero in probability. ∎

C.12
Proof of Corollary
3

The non-trivial part is to show that

\frac{1}{m^{2}\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left[{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right)-n\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}\right]=\frac{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})}{m^{2}\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right)-\frac{n}{m^{2}\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\boldsymbol{S}^{3}_{j,j}

(71)

converges to zero in probability under
WD
1
and M
1
.

Consider the first term on the right-hand side of (
71
). We have that

\frac{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})}{m^{2}\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\left(h^{2}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}+1\right)=\frac{{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})}{m^{2}}\left[\frac{h^{2}}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\right].

Notice that

\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}}{\sqrt{\tilde{r}(m)}}\left[\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}\right]^{1/2},

which is bounded because
m\leq\tilde{r}(m)
and
\frac{1}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}
has a finite limit. Also,
\frac{h^{2}}{\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}
is bounded.
Under
WD
1
and M
1
,
\frac{1}{m^{2}}{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{2})=\frac{1}{m^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges to zero because
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}
converges to a finite limit (see the Proof of Theorem
3
(i) ) and hence the first term on the right-hand side of (
71
) converges to zero under
WD
1
and M
1
.

Consider now the second term on the right-hand side of (
71
). We assumed that
\ell_{j}
is bounded and hence also
\underline{\ell}_{j}
; see (
14
). Also,
\tilde{r}({m})\geq m
. Therefore,

\frac{1}{m\tilde{r}(m)}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\underline{\ell}_{j}\boldsymbol{S}_{j,j}^{3}\leq\frac{C}{m^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\boldsymbol{S}_{j,j}^{3}=\frac{C}{m^{2}}{\rm tr}\left(\boldsymbol{S}^{3}\right).

It was shown in Lemma
5
that under
WD
1
and M
1
,
E\left[{\rm tr}(\boldsymbol{S}^{4})\right]/m^{2}\to 0
. It follows that
\frac{C}{m^{2}}{\rm tr}\left(\boldsymbol{S}^{3}\right)
converges to zero in
{\cal L}_{1}
and hence in probability. ∎

C.13
Proof of Theorem
9

We first consider an approximation for the LD scores. We have that

\ell_{j}=\sum_{p=1}^{m}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,p}^{2}=\frac{1}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}+f_{j}f_{p}]^{2}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}=\frac{1}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}]^{2}+2({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}f_{j}f_{p}+f_{j}^{2}f_{p}^{2}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}.

Now,

\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}]^{2}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}\leq\sum_{p=1}^{m}{[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}]^{2}}=\ell_{j,0}\leq C,

where in the last inequality we used the assumption that
\ell_{j,0}
is bounded. The mixed term is

2f_{j}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}f_{p}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}\leq 2f_{j}\sqrt{\sum_{p=1}^{m}[({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{0})_{j,p}]^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{f_{p}^{2}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}}=2f_{j}\sqrt{\ell_{j,0}}\sqrt{\sum_{p=1}^{m}\frac{f_{p}^{2}}{1+f_{p}^{2}}}\leq C\sqrt{m},

where the first inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz and the fact that
(1+f_{p}^{2})^{2}\geq 1+f_{p}^{2}
and the second inequality is true because
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\to C_{f}
and
\ell_{j,0},f_{j}^{2}
are bounded. It follows that

\left|\frac{\ell_{j}}{m}-\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}C_{f}\right|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{m}}.

(72)

Consider now
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}
.
By the definition of
\hat{Z}_{j,R}
in (
48
), we have that
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}=\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}
and by (
14
),

\underline{\ell}_{j}=\ell_{j}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{p=1}^{m}\left[{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,p})-1\right]
.
It follows that,

\hat{\ell}_{j,R}={\ell}_{j}+\frac{\sum_{p=1}^{m}\left[{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,p})-1\right]}{n}+\hat{Z}_{j,R},

and by Lemma
6
(i),

E\left(\hat{Z}_{j,R}\right)\leq\frac{C}{n}\left(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}+2{\ell}_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}{\rm tr}({\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}^{2})\right).

By similar arguments as above
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}_{j,j}^{3}
can be bounded by
C\|{\boldsymbol{f}}\|^{4}\leq Cm^{2}
; also
{\ell}_{j}^{2}\leq Cm^{2}
and

\frac{\sum_{p=1}^{m}[{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,p})-1]}{n}\leq C.

It follows that

E\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{{\ell}_{j}}{m}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{C}{m}.

(73)

We now compute the bias of
\hat{h}^{2}_{\text{LDSC-free}}
.
By the definition of
\hat{h}^{2}_{\text{LDSC-free}}
, which is given in (
29
), and by the conditional expectation in (
28
) we have that,

{\rm E}\left(\hat{h}^{2}_{\text{LDSC-free}}|{{\boldsymbol{X}}},{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right)=h^{2}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)^{2}}\\
+(1-\sigma_{\xi}^{2})\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right){d}_{j}^{2}}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)^{2}}+\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)\frac{1}{n}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)^{2}}:=({\rm I})+({\rm II})+({\rm III}).

(74)

Consider term (I) in (
74
),

({\rm I}):=h^{2}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)}{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}\right)^{2}}=h^{2}\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\left(\frac{n}{m}\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j}}{m}-\frac{d_{j}^{2}}{m}\right)}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}}.

(75)

Consider now the denominator of the last expression. We have that

{\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}-C^{2}_{f}\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)^{2}\right|\\
\leq\frac{n}{m}\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm E}\left|\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}-C_{f}^{2}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)^{2}\right|\\
=\frac{n}{m}\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm E}\left|\left[\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-C_{f}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-\left(\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}-C_{f}^{2}\right)\right]\left[\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}+C_{f}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}+\left(\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}+C_{f}^{2}\right)\right]\right|\\
\leq\frac{n}{m}\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sqrt{{\rm E}\left[\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-C_{f}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-\left(\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}-C_{f}^{2}\right)\right]^{2}}\sqrt{{\rm E}\left[\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}+C_{f}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}+\left(\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}+C_{f}^{2}\right)\right]^{2}}.

By (
72
) and (
73
), the last term is bounded by
\frac{C}{\sqrt{m}}
and therefore,

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}\to\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)^{2}C_{f}^{2}/\lambda.

(76)

in
{\cal L}_{1}
. By similar arguments, the numerator of (
75
) converges in
{\cal L}_{1}
to the same limit and hence term I converges in probability to
h^{2}
.

Consider now term (II) in (
74
). As in (
75
) we rewrite it

({\rm II})=(1-\sigma_{\xi}^{2})\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\frac{{d}_{j}^{2}}{m}}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}}.

The denominator has a finite limit as above and the numerator converges to zero in
{\cal L}_{1}
because

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}E\left|\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\frac{{d}_{j}^{2}}{m}\right|=E\left|\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\right|E\left(\frac{{d}_{j}^{2}}{m}\right)\leq\frac{C}{m}

where in the first equality we used the model assumption that the reference dataset (and hence
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}
) is independent of
d_{j}^{2}
and the last inequality is true because (
72
) and (
73
) imply that the first expectation is bounded and
E(d_{j}^{2})=1
as we assumed that the data is standardized. We conclude that term
II
converges to zero in probability.

Consider now term (III) in (
74
). Again we rewrite it

({\rm III})=\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)^{2}}.

(77)

We now consider approximating the term
\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}
by its expectation, which is

\frac{1}{mn}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}=\frac{1}{n}{\rm E}\left(-\sigma_{\xi}\sum_{i\in P_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}+\sigma_{\xi}\sum_{i\in P_{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}\right)^{2}.

The expectation of the random variable
\sum_{i\in P_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}
is
\frac{n}{2}\frac{-f_{j}}{\sqrt{1+f_{j}^{2}}}
. Its variance is
\frac{n}{2}
. Also,

{\rm E}\left(\sum_{i\in P_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}\sum_{i\in P_{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}\right)=-\frac{n^{2}}{4}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}.

It follows that

\frac{1}{mn}{\rm E}\left(-\sigma_{\xi}\sum_{i\in P_{1}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}+\sigma_{\xi}\sum_{i\in P_{2}}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}\right)^{2}=\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{nm(1+f_{j}^{2})}\left[2\times\left(\frac{n^{2}}{4}f_{j}^{2}+\frac{n}{2}\right)+2\frac{n^{2}}{4}f_{j}^{2}\right]\\
=\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\left(\frac{n}{m}f_{j}^{2}+\frac{1}{m}\right).

(78)

We next bound the variance of
\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}
. Notice that

{\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}
where
\eta_{i}:={\boldsymbol{X}}_{i,j}{\boldsymbol{\xi}}_{i}
. We have that
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{n}
are iid with finite fourth moment. Therefore,

{\rm Var}\left[\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{m^{2}n^{2}}{\rm Var}\left[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}}\eta_{i_{1}}\eta_{i_{2}}\right]=\frac{1}{m^{2}n^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}}{\rm Cov}(\eta_{i_{1}}\eta_{i_{2}},\eta_{i_{3}}\eta_{i_{4}})\leq\frac{C}{m},

(79)

where the last inequality is true because the covariance is different than zero when
i_{1}
is different from
i_{2},i_{3},i_{4}
, or symmetric cases.

Consider now the numerator of (
77
),
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}
. We have that

{\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}-\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}C_{f}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\right|\\
\leq{\rm E}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left|\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}-\frac{\mathaccentV{bar}016{\hat{\ell}}_{R}}{m}\right)-C_{f}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)\right|\frac{1}{mn}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}C_{f}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right){\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}-\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{n}{m}\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}\right|,

where in the inequality we used the model assumption that the reference dataset (and hence
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}
) is independent of
({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}
. The first term of the bound converges to zero due to (
72
) and (
73
) and the fact that
\frac{1}{mn}{\rm E}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}
is bounded. The second term of the bound converges to zero because of (
78
) and (
79
). It follows that
numerator of (
77
) converges in
{\cal L}_{1}
to

\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)^{2}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}C_{f}/\lambda.

It was already shown in (
76
) that the denominator of (
77
) converges in
{\cal L}_{1}
to

\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{f_{j}^{2}}{1+f_{j}^{2}}-C_{f}\right)^{2}C_{f}^{2}/\lambda

and hence term III converges in probability to
\sigma_{\xi}^{2}/C_{f}
. We conclude that

{\rm E}\left(\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC-free}\mid{\boldsymbol{X}},{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}h^{2}+\sigma_{\xi}^{2}/C_{f},

as
m,n\to\infty
.

To show that
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC}
has the same asymptotic, we have similar to to the calculation in (
74
) that

{\rm E}\left(\hat{h}^{2}_{\text{LDSC}}|{{\boldsymbol{X}}},{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{R},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\right)=h^{2}\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\left(\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}\right)}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}\\
+(1-\sigma_{\xi}^{2})\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}{d}_{j}^{2}}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}+\frac{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\frac{1}{n}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}}{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}}:=A+B+C.

For term
A
, one can show by using similar arguments to those used in the calculation of term
I
in (
74
) that

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}\left(\frac{\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j}-d_{j}^{2}}{m}\right)\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}\right)^{2}

both converge in
{\cal L}_{1}
to

\frac{C_{f}^{2}}{\lambda}\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{f_{j}^{4}}{(1+f_{j}^{2})^{2}},

and hence term
A
converges in probability to
h^{2}
. Term
B
converges in probability to 0 as term
II
in (
74
) and for term
C
we have that

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}\frac{1}{mn}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{j}^{T}{\boldsymbol{\xi}})^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal L}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\frac{C_{f}}{\lambda}\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{f_{j}^{4}}{(1+f_{j}^{2})^{2}}

and that

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left(\frac{\hat{\ell}_{j,R}}{m}\right)^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal L}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{C_{f}^{2}}{\lambda}\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{f_{j}^{4}}{(1+f_{j}^{2})^{2}}.

Therefore, term
C
converges in probability to
\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{C_{f}}
. It follows that
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC}
has the same asymptotic bias as
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC-free}
. The proof that
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm GWASH}
has the same asymptotic bias is similar and hence omitted. ∎

C.14
Proof of Theorem
10

Proof of Part (i):
Consider the denominator of
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC-W}
, as defined in (
20
), which we denote by
D_{\rm LDSC-W}
and recall that
w_{j}=g\left(\hat{h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}\right)
. Thus,

D_{\rm LDSC-W}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left(\hat{h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}.

(80)

We want to replace
\hat{h}^{2}
with
h^{2}
in the above expression.
By the mean value theorem applied to the function
g
and the bound (
30
) on its derivative with respect to its first argument, we have

\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left(\hat{h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g({h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R})\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right|\leq C\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}|\hat{h}^{2}-h^{2}|.

(81)

We have that
\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}=I(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\leq 1)+I(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}>1)\hat{\ell}_{j,R}
. It follows that
{\rm E}\left(\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right)
is bounded if
{\rm E}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{4}\right)
is bounded. Indeed, by the definition of
\hat{Z}_{j,R}
in (
48
),

\hat{\ell}_{j,R}=\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}
and
{\rm E}(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{4})
and
\underline{\ell}_{j}
are bounded by Lemma
6
(ii) and WD
2
, respectively (recall that in the Gaussian case
\underline{\ell}_{j}=\ell_{j}(1+1/n)
). Thus,
{\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{n}{m}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}^{4}\right|
is bounded.
By the consistency assumption on
\hat{h}^{2}
in the statement of the theorem, we have that
|\hat{h}^{2}-h^{2}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0
, and thus, the difference in (
81
) converges to 0 in probability.

We now aim at replacing
\underline{\ell}_{j,R}
with
\underline{\ell}_{j}
in (
80
). Again, by the mean value theorem applied to the function
g
and the bound (
30
) on its derivative with respect to its second argument, we have

\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right|\leq C\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}-\underline{\ell}_{j}|\frac{n}{m}{\ell}^{*}_{j,R}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2},

(82)

where
{\ell}^{*}_{j,R}
is between
\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}
and
\underline{\ell}_{j}
. Recall that
\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}:=\max\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R},1\right)
and that
\underline{\ell}_{j}=\ell_{j}(1+1/n)
and
\ell_{j}\geq 1
. It follows that

|\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}-\underline{\ell}_{j}|\leq|\hat{\ell}_{j,R}-\underline{\ell}_{j}|\leq|\hat{Z}_{j,R}|,

where the last inequality is true because
\hat{\ell}_{j,R}=\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}
. It also implies that
|{\ell}^{*}_{j,R}|\leq|\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}|
.
Hence, continuing to bound the expression in (
82
) we have that

\displaystyle{\rm E}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}|\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}-\underline{\ell}_{j}|{\ell}^{*}_{j,R}\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{m}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\rm E}\left(|\hat{Z}_{j,R}||\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}|\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left[{\rm E}\left(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{4}\right){\rm E}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{4}\right)\right]^{1/2},

where the last inequality is true due to Cauchy-Schwartz and the assumption that
\underline{\ell}_{j}
is bounded. We have that
{\rm E}(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{4})\leq C/m
(see the proof of Theorem
5
) and that
{\rm E}\left(\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{4}\right)
is bounded (see the argument after (
81
)). Therefore, the difference in (
82
) converges to zero in
{\cal L}_{1}
and hence also in probability.

Thus, it was shown that

\left|D_{\rm LDSC-W}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

The subtracting term inside the absolute value converges in
{\cal L}_{2}
(and hence also in probability) to
\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}g(h^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j})\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}/\lambda
because

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\frac{n}{m}\hat{\ell}_{j,R}^{2}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\frac{n}{m}\left(\underline{\ell}_{j}+\hat{Z}_{j,R}\right)^{2},

and we have that
{\rm E}(\hat{Z}_{j,R}^{4})\leq C/m
and
\underline{\ell}_{j}
is bounded and
m/n\to\lambda
.

Consider now the numerator of
\hat{h}^{2}_{\rm LDSC-W}
, which is

N_{\rm LDSC-W}:=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left(\hat{h}^{2},\mathaccentV{bar}016{\ell}_{j,R}\right)\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\left(u_{j}^{2}-1\right).

By similar arguments, we have that

\left|N_{\rm LDSC-W}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\left(u_{j}^{2}-1\right)\right|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}0.

Now, a straightforward extension of the proof of Theorem
5
(ii) shows that

\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}g\left({h}^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j}\right)\hat{\ell}_{j,R}\left(u_{j}^{2}-1\right)~{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\cal L}_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}}~h^{2}\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j}g(h^{2},\underline{\ell}_{j})\underline{\ell}_{j}^{2}/\lambda.

It follows that
\hat{h}_{\text{\rm LDSC-W}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle P}}{{\longrightarrow}}h^{2}
as
m,n\to\infty
.

The proof of Part (ii) is similar to that of Part (i) using arguments from the proof of Theorem
5
; hence, it is omitted. ∎
